        Driving it away with `g7g5` seems very strong.
        But `f7f6` is very solid.

        A bishop at c3 attacks d2, e1 and b4, a5.

        Now the Bishop at c3 is attacking f6 and h8.
        Wait, if the pawn moves to g5, it's not on g7 anymore.
        The Bishop at c3 was attacking g7. By moving `g7g5`, Black removes the target.
        This is also a benefit!

        Wait, let's check `f7f6` again.
        If Black plays `f7f6`, the pawn moves to f6.
        Now the Bishop at c3 is attacking f6!
        So `f7f6` puts a pawn on a square that the Bishop attacks.
        The pawn at f6 would be defended by the King at g8 and the Rook at f8.
        So `Bxf6` would be `Bxf6`, `Rxf6` or `Kxf6`.
        White loses a Bishop for a pawn. Not good for White.

        So both `g7g5` and `f7f6` are safe.
        But `g7g5` is more active.
